CHROME EXTENSION BUILD GUIDE




SecondLook
A field manual for a security suite disguised as quality-of-
life tools.




We don’t tell you that the internet is dangerous. We
tell you when something deserves a second look.
Every file, every rule, and every trade-off — from
empty folder to store listing, in plain JavaScript with
no build step.


Parts 0 – 13, complete
Manifest V3 · ten modules · one verdict engine · local-first, zero telemetry

SecondLook - Build Guide                                                                                                                      4
Part 0 - Foundation & Architecture

0.1 What you are building
SecondLook is a Manifest V3 Chrome extension that ships as a suite of ten "quality of life" tools and quietly behaves like a
security product. The pitch to users is honest but soft-spoken: We don't tell you that the internet is dangerous. We tell you
when something deserves a second look. Hover a link and a small pill tells you whether the destination looks normal. Land
on a page with an invisible click-stealing overlay and the overlay simply stops working. Start typing a password into a form
that posts it to a different domain than the one you are looking at, and a full-page interception stops the submit until you
consciously allow it. None of it phones home, none of it uploads anything, and every single intervention can be undone.

The suite is organized into four pillars. Link & Site Intel contains Link Sniper (hover verdicts on links), Redirect Detective
(follows redirect chains and shows you every hop), and Trust Badge (flags trust-seal images that are just pictures). Form &
Click Safety contains Form Guardian (credential exfiltration on submit), Ghost Click Blocker (invisible full-page overlays),
and Fake Download Button (competing download clusters). Files & Downloads contains Download Guard (magic-byte
sniffing of downloaded files). Annoyance & Privacy contains Tab Guard (popup-storm containment), Cookie Tamer (parks
consent banners without ever clicking "Accept"), and Webcam/Mic Indicator (a persistent chip whenever a page uses your
camera or microphone). Each module can be turned off independently, per site or globally, and turning a module off
genuinely removes its code from every page.

This guide is written for a developer with solid modern JavaScript who has never shipped a Chrome extension. Everything is
plain ES2020+ JavaScript, CSS and HTML: no bundler, no framework, no build step, no external dependencies at runtime.
You will paste every file in this guide from scratch, in order, and at the end of each part the extension will load and run in
chrome://extensions. Budget roughly four weeks: Part 0 is a full weekend, Parts 1 through 10 are about a day each, and
Parts 11 through 13 close out packaging and store listing.


0.2 Architecture at 10,000 feet
The extension has exactly one brain and many hands. The service worker (the MV3 "background script") owns every
privileged decision: it hosts the verdict engine, mediates all message routing, watches downloads and tab creation, and owns
the settings store. Content scripts are deliberately dumb: they observe the page, ask the service worker for verdicts over the
message bus, and render UI with the shared component kit. The engine is the only component in the entire codebase allowed
to score a URL - modules may collect evidence and escalate context, but nobody rolls their own risk numbers.

  (architecture overview)
                        +--------------------------------------+
                        |        chrome:// (privileged)        |
                        | storage.local storage.session        |
        writes/reads    | scripting tabs downloads cookies|
  +---------------------+--------------------------------------+---------+
  |                     |                                                  |
  |   +--------------+ v         +---------------------------+           |
  |   | ui/popup     | runtime   | background/               |           |
  |   | ui/options | messages | service-worker.js            |           |
  |   | ui/dashboard|<--------->|    module-router.js        |           |
  |   +--------------+           |   bootstrap.js            |           |
  |                              |   tab-guard / download-   |           |
  | extension pages              |   guard / safe-mode ...   |           |
  | (ES pages, same              +------------+--------------+           |
  |   script files)                           | hosts                    |
  |                                           v                          |
  |                              +---------------------------+           |
  |                              | engine/                   |           |
  |                              |   verdict-engine.js       |           |
  |                              |   url-analyzer.js         |           |
  |                              |   domain-intel.js         |           |
  |                              |   known-brands.js         |           |

  |                              |   redirect-resolver.js    |             |
  |                              +---------------------------+             |
  +-----------------------------------------------------------------|-----+
                                                                     |
  http(s) page tabs                                                |
  +-----------------------------------------------------------------+-----+
  | ISOLATED world                           MAIN world (only P10) |
  | +---------------------------------+      +------------------------+ |
  | | modules/<module>/<module>.js     |     | av-indicator/           | |
  | | shared/messaging.js cs-bridge |        | main-world.js           | |
  | | ui/toast.js verdict-card.js      |<----+ hooks getUserMedia      | |
  | +---------------------------------+ post +------------------------+ |
  | verdict requests go to the SW        message                         |
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+

The vertical split matters more than any individual box. Everything above the second horizontal rule runs with extension
privileges; everything below it runs inside web pages. The only bridge between the two worlds is the message contract
{module, type, payload} wrapped in promises by shared/messaging.js. Data flows in one of three patterns. Ask: a
content script sends engine / analyze with a URL and gets back a verdict object, which it caches per page load. Report: a
content script sends <module> / status with a one-line summary that the popup later reads from session storage for the tab.
Push: the service worker broadcasts core / moduleToggled to every open tab when a setting changes, and targeted
messages such as redirectDetective / showChain when a context-menu action resolves.

All shared code uses a single convention: every file is a classic script that attaches its API to the globalThis.SL namespace
and guards against double execution. The service worker loads shared files with importScripts(), extension pages load
them with <script src> tags, and the bootstrap registers them into content scripts by path. One file, three runtimes, zero
module-system gymnastics - this is the payoff for avoiding ES modules, and it is why the codebase has no build step.


0.3 The verdict contract
Every judgment the extension ever shows belongs to one of three words, and every module must use the engine's vocabulary
instead of inventing its own. The words are chosen so that a non-technical user can act on them without reading anything else:

 Verdict             Color & weight             Meaning                                                    User action

 CLEAR               Green pill                 Nothing unusual for this URL in this context               None - keep browsing

 SECOND              Amber pill + one plain     Some signal deserves attention before you click, submit,   Pause a beat, read the one
 LOOK                sentence                   or trust                                                   sentence

 INTERCEPTE          Red card, full attention   High-confidence credential or file exfiltration in         Actively choose Stop or
 D                                              progress                                                   Continue


Three rules make the system trustworthy. First, one visible warning per event: a link hover shows at most one pill, a page
shows at most one banner; the engine ranks reasons internally and surfaces only the strongest plain-language sentence. Second,
every interception is reversible: INTERCEPTED is always a card with a Stop and a Continue button, never a silent block -
if we stop a submit, we keep the form state and re-dispatch it on request. Third, when in doubt, suggest, never interrupt:
the engine's URL-only score is deliberately capped below the interception threshold (39 of 40 required), so no combination of
URL signals alone can stop the user. Only modules with hard evidence - Form Guardian seeing a password leave the visible
origin, Download Guard seeing a file whose bytes contradict its extension - are allowed to pass escalate: true and cross
the red line.


0.4 "OFF must be real OFF"
Most extensions treat their toggle as a UI preference: the warnings disappear but the code keeps running. SecondLook treats
the toggle as a contract with three clauses, and Part 0 implements the machinery for all of them. Clause 1 - no static
injection: the manifest's content_scripts field is empty, permanently. All module code enters pages only through


SecondLook - Build Guide                                                                                                                6
chrome.scripting.registerContentScripts() calls made by the bootstrap at install, at browser startup, and after
settings changes. Clause 2 - off means unregistered: flipping a module's toggle writes settings, then the service worker
unregisters that module's script ids and clears any DNR session rules the module owned. Clause 3 - open tabs get cleaned
up: unregistration does not touch already-loaded pages, so the router also broadcasts a teardown message; the module's
content script runs its registered cleanup functions (remove pills, banners and overlays; restore pointer-events; detach
observers) and then removes itself from the page.

  (toggle lifecycle - the whole story in one diagram)
 User flips toggle in popup
        |
        v
 popup.js: SL.msg.send('core','setModuleEnabled',{moduleId,enabled:false})
        |   (promise; row shows a subtle 'syncing' pulse until reply)
        v
 module-router.js: SL.Settings.setModuleEnabled(...)
        |   writes chrome.storage.local('sl.settings')
        v
 storage.onChanged fires in the service worker
        |
        v
 bootstrap.js: reconcile('settings-changed')
        |   1. desired = enabled modules with script specs
        |   2. current = chrome.scripting.getRegisteredContentScripts()
        |   3. unregister ids that are no longer desired
        |   4. register ids that became desired (duplicate-safe)
        v
 router broadcasts to every open http/https tab:
        {module:'core', type:'moduleToggled', payload:{moduleId,enabled:false}}
        |
        v
 cs-bridge.js in each tab: this module's script matches ->
        run SL.cleanupFor(moduleId):
        - remove injected DOM (pills, banners, chips, intercept layer)
        - restore modified styles (pointer-events: auto)
        - disconnect observers and event listeners
        - set module state to 'dead'; no further page mutation
        |
        v
 session status lines for that module are dropped from every tab

Turning a module back on is the mirror image: reconcile registers the script ids, and for pages already open (which will never
see     the    registered    script    because     they     finished     loading),     bootstrap.injectIntoOpenTabs()               calls
chrome.scripting.executeScript and insertCSS directly. The result is that toggling feels instant in both directions on
every page you have open - which is also the single best demo of the product's honesty, and exactly what
test-pages/toggle-test.html (Part 12) lets you show off.

Site-level exceptions are the one place where "real off" gets a caveat, so it is called out here rather than buried. A per-site
disable keeps the module's scripts registered globally (because dynamic registration is pattern-based, not host-list based) but
the module's bridge checks the effective settings for the current host at boot and stays completely dormant: no observers, no
listeners on the page, no DOM. The service worker applies the same check before any privileged action for that site. The
privacy promise is identical - zero page interaction - but the script file is technically present in the page's isolated world. If you
want physical absence on a specific site, the global toggle is the real switch.

   DEVIATION NOTE - MV3 content-script registration cannot be scoped to a runtime-computed host allowlist without
   re-registering on every change. We accept dormant-but-present scripts for per-site disables; global disables physically unregister.
   This is documented in the README (Part 13) as the honest privacy statement.

SecondLook - Build Guide                                                                                                            8
0.6 manifest.json
The manifest is the product's contract with Chrome, and every line here is load-bearing. Note especially the empty
content_scripts array: that single design choice is what makes "OFF is real OFF" mechanically true, because Chrome
itself will never inject our module code into a page - only the bootstrap will. The service worker is declared as a classic script
on purpose: we use importScripts() so that the exact same shared files that run in content scripts and extension pages also
run in the worker, under the identical globalThis.SL namespace.

    manifest.json
{
    "manifest_version": 3,
    "name": "SecondLook",
    "version": "1.0.0",
    "description": "Quality-of-life tools that quietly watch your back. " +
      "Local-only, zero telemetry, every warning undoable.",
    "minimum_chrome_version": "116",

    "background": {
       "service_worker": "background/service-worker.js"
    },

    "action": {
       "default_popup": "ui/popup.html",
       "default_title": "SecondLook",
       "default_icon": {
         "16": "assets/icons/icon-16.png",
         "32": "assets/icons/icon-32.png",
         "48": "assets/icons/icon-48.png",
         "128": "assets/icons/icon-128.png"
       }
    },

    "options_page": "ui/options.html",

    "icons": {
       "16": "assets/icons/icon-16.png",
       "32": "assets/icons/icon-32.png",
       "48": "assets/icons/icon-48.png",
       "128": "assets/icons/icon-128.png"
    },

    "permissions": [
       "storage", "tabs", "scripting", "downloads", "cookies",
       "alarms", "contextMenus", "tabGroups",
       "declarativeNetRequest", "webRequest"
    ],

    "host_permissions": ["http://*/*", "https://*/*"],

    "content_scripts": [],

    "web_accessible_resources": []
}

The content_scripts: [] entry is deliberate and self-documenting: a reviewer of your store listing can see at a glance that
no module code is statically injected. The empty web_accessible_resources matters just as much - nothing from the
package is exposed to web pages, which removes an entire class of fingerprinting and abuse concerns during store review.
Every icon referenced here is generated by tools/make-icons.py at the end of this part.


Permission justifications (keep this table - you will reuse it verbatim in the Web Store review form in
Part 13)




SecondLook - Build Guide                                                                                                         9
   Permission                    Why it exists                                                                    Used by

   storage                       settings, local stats, session runtime state, salt                               all

   tabs                          current-tab host for popup + effective settings; Tab Guard                       popup, P8

   scripting                     dynamic register/unregister of module scripts; inject into open tabs             bootstrap

   downloads                     observe + cancel + retry downloads for magic-byte check                          P7

   cookies                       site sweep that preserves login cookies                                          P9

   alarms                        daily prune of the local event log                                               service worker

   contextMenus                  "Where does this link really go?", "Safe Mode this tab"                          P0, P2

   tabGroups                     isolation group for popup storms                                                 P8

   declarativeNetRequest         Safe Mode per-tab session rules (the ONLY blocking API)                          P0

   webRequest                    observe redirect hops by requestId (never blocking)                              P2, engine

   http/https host               run analysis on every page the user browses; sniff download bytes                all modules


Two of these deserve a sentence of honesty before a reviewer asks. The broad host permission exists because the product is
site-agnostic by definition - a link checker that only runs on whitelisted sites protects nobody - and all analysis is on-device, so
the permission grants access but never sends data anywhere. webRequest is observer-only: the manifest does not include
webRequestBlocking, which MV3 would reject for store extensions anyway, so the API is used purely to number and record
redirect hops while the service worker's own fetch follows them.

   WARNING - The spec originally listed tabGroups implicitly through Tab Guard's design; it must be declared explicitly or
   chrome.tabGroups is undefined at runtime and popup-storm containment silently fails. It is included above - just do not
   "optimize" it away later.


   DEVIATION NOTE - The original design sketch pinned host permissions to specific sites (mail providers, search engines). That
   would make always-on modules impossible: link verdicts and form checks are needed everywhere, not on three domains. Broad
   hosts with local-only processing is the honest trade-off, and Part 13 words the store justification accordingly. Mail providers and
   search engines still get special-cased in code: Form Guardian exempts mail hosts, Cookie Tamer preserves their login cookies,
   Redirect Detective resolves their shortlinks.




0.7 The shared layer
Five files make up the shared layer, and every runtime in the extension loads at least three of them. They follow one
convention throughout: an IIFE, an idempotency guard, and an attachment to globalThis.SL. Because they are classic
scripts, the identical bytes run in the service worker (via importScripts), in extension pages (via <script src>), and in
content scripts (via registration js arrays). The only file that is content-world-specific is cs-bridge.js; everything else is
world-neutral.


shared/storage.js
Thin promise wrappers over chrome.storage.local and chrome.storage.session, plus the three persistent data
structures SecondLook keeps: a stats counter, a capped event log, and the salt used by Form Guardian's domain hashing. One
privacy rule is enforced here at the storage boundary rather than trusted to callers: the event log persists hostnames only,
never full URLs - so even your local history of what SecondLook flagged cannot be reconstructed into a browsing history.




SecondLook - Build Guide                                                                                                                 10
  shared/storage.js
/* shared/storage.js - storage, stats, event log, salt.
 * Classic script, attaches SL.Storage; safe to load in any runtime. */
(function (global) {
  'use strict';
  const root = global.SL = global.SL || {};
  if (root.Storage) return;

  const EVENTS_KEY = 'sl.events';
  const EVENTS_MAX = 200;   // tuning: local event-log cap
  const STATS_KEY = 'sl.stats';
  const SALT_KEY = 'sl.salt';

  async function localGet(key, fallback) {
    const got = await chrome.storage.local.get(key);
    return (key in got && got[key] !== undefined) ? got[key] : fallback;
  }

  async function localSet(key, value) {
    await chrome.storage.local.set({ [key]: value });
  }

  /* storage.session is reachable from the SW and extension pages only.
    * Content scripts report through SL.msg instead - by design, so no
    * setAccessLevel call ever widens session data to page worlds. */
  async function sessionGet(key, fallback) {
     if (!chrome.storage.session) return fallback;
     const got = await chrome.storage.session.get(key);
     return (key in got && got[key] !== undefined) ? got[key] : fallback;
  }

  async function sessionSet(key, value) {
    if (!chrome.storage.session) return;
    await chrome.storage.session.set({ [key]: value });
  }

  async function getStats() {
    return localGet(STATS_KEY, {
      byVerdict: { CLEAR: 0, SECOND_LOOK: 0, INTERCEPTED: 0 },
      byModule: {}
    });
  }

  async function bumpStat(moduleId, verdict) {
    const stats = await getStats();
    if (verdict && stats.byVerdict[verdict] !== undefined) {
      stats.byVerdict[verdict] += 1;
    }
    stats.byModule[moduleId] = (stats.byModule[moduleId] || 0) + 1;
    await localSet(STATS_KEY, stats);
  }

  /* Privacy boundary: events persist the HOST only, never the full URL,
    * so the local log can never be reconstructed into a browsing history. */
  function hostOf(url) {
     try { return new URL(url).hostname; } catch (e) { return ''; }
  }

  async function pushEvent(evt) {
    const list = await localGet(EVENTS_KEY, []);
    list.unshift({
      ts: Date.now(),
      module: evt.module || '?',
      verdict: evt.verdict || null,
      host: hostOf(evt.url || ''),        // hostname only, by design
      note: evt.note || ''
    });
    await localSet(EVENTS_KEY, list.slice(0, EVENTS_MAX));
  }

  async function getEvents(limit) {
    const list = await localGet(EVENTS_KEY, []);
    return typeof limit === 'number' ? list.slice(0, limit) : list;



SecondLook - Build Guide                                                        11
  }

  async function pruneEvents() {
    const list = await localGet(EVENTS_KEY, []);
    if (list.length && list.length < EVENTS_MAX * 0.75) return 0;
    await localSet(EVENTS_KEY, list.slice(0, EVENTS_MAX));
    return list.length - EVENTS_MAX;
  }

  /* Random per-install salt; used by Form Guardian to store salted
    * hashes of approved origins instead of the origins themselves. */
  async function ensureSalt() {
     let salt = await localGet(SALT_KEY, null);
     if (!salt) {
       const bytes = new Uint8Array(16);
       crypto.getRandomValues(bytes);
       salt = Array.from(bytes, (b) => b.toString(16).padStart(2, '0')).join('');
       await localSet(SALT_KEY, salt);
     }
     return salt;
  }

  async function resetData() {
    await chrome.storage.local.remove([EVENTS_KEY, STATS_KEY]);
  }

  root.Storage = {
     localGet, localSet, sessionGet, sessionSet,
     getStats, bumpStat, pushEvent, getEvents, pruneEvents,
     ensureSalt, resetData,
     EVENTS_MAX
  };
})(globalThis);



shared/settings.js
The settings file defines the schema for all ten modules plus the global switch and per-site overrides, and it is the single place
that knows how to compute an *effective* configuration for a given host. The deep-merge in effective() is what lets each
layer (defaults, then global settings, then the site override) speak in terms of the same flat module keys. Every consumer in the
codebase - bootstrap, router, content bridge, popup, options - goes through this file; nobody reads raw settings objects and
improvises.

  shared/settings.js
/* shared/settings.js - settings schema + effective(host) resolution. */
(function (global) {
  'use strict';
  const root = global.SL = global.SL || {};
  if (root.Settings) return;

  const KEY = 'sl.settings';

  const DEFAULTS = {
     globalEnabled: true,
     modules: {
        demoGreeting:      { enabled: true },                      // dev only
        linkSniper:        { enabled: true, hoverDelayMs: 350, showClear: true },
        redirectDetective: { enabled: true, maxHops: 8 },
        trustBadge:        { enabled: true },
        formGuardian:      { enabled: true, rememberApproved: true },
        ghostClick:        { enabled: true, annotate: false },
        fakeDownload:      { enabled: true },
        downloadGuard:     { enabled: true, blockOnMismatch: true },
        tabGuard:          { enabled: true, threshold: 3, windowMs: 10000 },
        cookieTamer:       { enabled: true, strategy: 'prefer-reject' },
        avIndicator:       { enabled: true }
     },
     siteOverrides: {}    // host -> { modules: { moduleId: false } }
  };

  function clone(obj) { return JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(obj)); }



SecondLook - Build Guide                                                                                                        12
  function mergeInto(base, patch) {
    for (const k of Object.keys(patch || {})) {
      const v = patch[k];
      if (v && typeof v === 'object' && !Array.isArray(v) &&
          base[k] && typeof base[k] === 'object' && !Array.isArray(base[k])) {
        mergeInto(base[k], v);
      } else if (v !== undefined) {
        base[k] = v;
      }
    }
    return base;
  }

  async function get() {
    const stored = await root.Storage.localGet(KEY, null);
    if (!stored) return clone(DEFAULTS);
    // unknown-module-proof: defaults win for anything missing
    return mergeInto(clone(DEFAULTS), stored);
  }

  async function save(next) {
    await root.Storage.localSet(KEY, next);
    return next;
  }

  async function setModuleEnabled(moduleId, enabled) {
    const s = await get();
    if (!s.modules[moduleId]) s.modules[moduleId] = { enabled };
    else s.modules[moduleId].enabled = !!enabled;
    return save(s);
  }

  async function setModuleOption(moduleId, option, value) {
    const s = await get();
    if (!s.modules[moduleId]) s.modules[moduleId] = {};
    s.modules[moduleId][option] = value;
    return save(s);
  }

  async function setGlobalEnabled(enabled) {
    const s = await get();
    s.globalEnabled = !!enabled;
    return save(s);
  }

  async function setSiteOverride(host, moduleId, enabled) {
    const s = await get();
    if (!s.siteOverrides[host]) s.siteOverrides[host] = { modules: {} };
    s.siteOverrides[host].modules[moduleId] = !!enabled;
    if (!Object.keys(s.siteOverrides[host].modules).length) {
      delete s.siteOverrides[host];
    }
    return save(s);
  }

  async function clearSiteOverride(host, moduleId) {
    const s = await get();
    const site = s.siteOverrides[host];
    if (site && site.modules && moduleId in site.modules) {
      delete site.modules[moduleId];
      if (!Object.keys(site.modules).length) delete s.siteOverrides[host];
    }
    return save(s);
  }

  async function clearAllSiteOverrides(host) {
    const s = await get();
    delete s.siteOverrides[host];
    return save(s);
  }

  /* Effective settings for one host: defaults <- global <- site override. */
  async function effective(host) {
    const s = await get();



SecondLook - Build Guide                                                         13
      const out = clone(s);
      const site = host && s.siteOverrides && s.siteOverrides[host];
      if (site && site.modules) {
        for (const [modId, enabled] of Object.entries(site.modules)) {
          if (!out.modules[modId]) out.modules[modId] = {};
          out.modules[modId].enabled = enabled;   // site layer wins
        }
      }
      return out;
  }

  function isModuleOn(settings, moduleId, host) {
    if (!settings || settings.globalEnabled === false) return false;
    const site = host && settings.siteOverrides &&
      settings.siteOverrides[host];
    const siteFlag = site && site.modules && site.modules[moduleId];
    if (siteFlag !== undefined && siteFlag !== null) return !!siteFlag;
    const m = settings.modules && settings.modules[moduleId];
    return !!m && m.enabled !== false;
  }

  /* Subscribe to settings changes (chrome.storage.onChanged). */
  function subscribe(cb) {
    chrome.storage.onChanged.addListener((changes, area) => {
      if (area === 'local' && changes[KEY]) cb(changes[KEY].newValue,
                                               changes[KEY].oldValue);
    });
  }

  root.Settings = {
     KEY, DEFAULTS, get, save, setModuleEnabled, setModuleOption,
     setGlobalEnabled, setSiteOverride, clearSiteOverride,
     clearAllSiteOverrides, effective, isModuleOn, subscribe
  };
})(globalThis);



shared/messaging.js
The message bus wraps chrome.runtime.sendMessage in promises and normalizes responses to {ok: true, data} or
{ok: false, error}. The listen() helper exists for the content world and extension pages: it filters messages addressed to
one module id (or to '*' for broadcasts) and always returns true from the listener callback so the response channel stays
open for the async reply. The service worker does not use listen() - it has the router, which is deliberately fatter and lives in
background/.

  shared/messaging.js
/* shared/messaging.js - promise message bus. Message shape:
 *    {module, type, payload} -> {ok:true, data} | {ok:false, error} */
(function (global) {
   'use strict';
   const root = global.SL = global.SL || {};
   if (root.msg) return;

  function send(module, type, payload) {
    return new Promise((resolve) => {
      try {
        chrome.runtime.sendMessage({ module, type, payload }, (res) => {
          // Absorb "Receiving end does not exist" (SW asleep or no listener)
          void chrome.runtime.lastError;
          resolve(res && typeof res === 'object' ? res
                  : { ok: false, error: 'no-response' });
        });
      } catch (err) {
        resolve({ ok: false, error: String(err) });
      }
    });
  }

  /* Handler -> may return plain data (wrapped as {ok:true,data}) or a
   * full response object. Never throws across the boundary. */
  function listen(moduleId, handler) {



SecondLook - Build Guide                                                                                                       14
      chrome.runtime.onMessage.addListener((msg, sender, sendResponse) => {
        if (!msg || typeof msg !== 'object' || typeof msg.type !== 'string') {
          return false;
        }
        if (msg.module !== moduleId && msg.module !== '*') return false;
        Promise.resolve(handler(msg, sender))
          .then((data) => {
             sendResponse(data && data.__response ? data
                          : { ok: true, data: data === undefined ? null : data });
          })
          .catch((err) => {
             sendResponse({ ok: false, error: String(err && err.message || err) });
          });
        return true;    // keep the channel open for the async sendResponse
      });
  }

  root.msg = { send, listen };
})(globalThis);



shared/module-registry.js
The registry is pure data - no chrome.* calls at import time, which is what makes it safe to load in every runtime including
the popup. Each module entry carries its catalog information (name, pillar, one-line blurb, default) and, once the part that
builds it arrives, its content-script specifications. In Part 0 only the demo module has scripts; Parts 1-10 each show the exact
entry to paste into MODULES as that module comes online. This is also the single source of truth for the popup's ten rows, so the
UI never drifts from what the bootstrap actually registers.

  shared/module-registry.js
/* shared/module-registry.js - catalog + content-script specs.
 * PURE DATA: no chrome.* access at load time, safe in every runtime. */
(function (global) {
  'use strict';
  const root = global.SL = global.SL || {};
  if (root.Registry) return;

  const PILLARS = [
     { id: 'linkIntel',      name: 'Link & Site Intel' },

     'shared/messaging.js',

  /* Registration spec fields:
   *    id         unique script id used by chrome.scripting (persisted!)
   *    matches    URL patterns this script set runs on
   *    js/css     files injected, root-relative paths
   *    runAt      document_start | document_end | document_idle
   *    world      ISOLATED (default) | MAIN (only av-indicator uses MAIN)

  const MODULES = [

         css: [...BASE_CSS, 'modules/demo-greeting/demo-greeting.css']



SecondLook - Build Guide                                                                                                       15

     },

        defaultEnabled: true, scripts: null },          // <- Part 8 (SW-only)
     { id: 'cookieTamer', name: 'Cookie Tamer', pillar: 'annoyance',
        blurb: 'Parks consent banners; never clicks Accept.',
        defaultEnabled: true, scripts: null },          // <- Part 9
     { id: 'avIndicator', name: 'Webcam/Mic Indicator', pillar: 'annoyance',
        blurb: 'A chip whenever camera or mic is in use.',
        defaultEnabled: true, scripts: null }           // <- Part 10
  ];

  function byId(id) {
    return MODULES.find((m) => m.id === id) || null;
  }

  function allScriptSpecs() {
    const out = [];
    for (const m of MODULES) if (m.scripts) out.push(...m.scripts);
    return out;
  }

  function scriptIdsFor(moduleId) {
    const m = byId(moduleId);
    return m && m.scripts ? m.scripts.map((s) => s.id) : [];
  }

  root.Registry = {
     PILLARS, MODULES, byId, allScriptSpecs, scriptIdsFor,
     BRIDGE_FILES, BASE_CSS
  };
})(globalThis);



shared/cs-bridge.js
The bridge is the content world's runtime and the enforcement point for the toggle lifecycle inside pages. A module never
wires its own listeners for settings changes or teardown - it calls SL.boot(moduleId, impl) with up to four hooks
(onEnabled, onConfig, onDisabled, onMessage) and registers undo functions with SL.registerCleanup(). The bridge
owns the verdict cache (so ten modules hovering the same link issue one analysis), the status reporter, and the page host
calculation that per-site overrides key on.

  shared/cs-bridge.js
/* shared/cs-bridge.js - content-world runtime for all modules.
 * Modules call SL.boot(id, impl); the bridge owns lifecycle. */
(function (global) {
  'use strict';
  const root = global.SL = global.SL || {};
  if (root.__bridge) return;



SecondLook - Build Guide                                                                                               16
  const impls = {};      // moduleId -> implementation hooks
  const cleanups = {};   // moduleId -> [undo functions]
  const alive = {};      // moduleId -> currently active on this page
  const VERDICT_CACHE_MAX = 300;

  let pageHost = '';
  try { pageHost = location.hostname || ''; } catch (e) { /* non-http(s) */ }

  function host() { return pageHost; }

  function send(module, type, payload) {
    return root.msg.send(module, type, payload);
  }

  /* ---- verdict cache: one analysis per URL per page load ---------- */
  const verdictCache = new Map();
  const verdictPending = new Map();

  function verdictKey(url, ctx) {
    const c = ctx || {};
    return url + '|' + (c.credentials ? 'c' : '') +
           (c.escalate ? 'e' : '') + (c.resolvedUrl ? 'r' : '');
  }

  async function verdict(url, ctx) {
    const key = verdictKey(url, ctx);
    if (verdictCache.has(key)) return verdictCache.get(key);
    if (verdictPending.has(key)) return verdictPending.get(key);
    const p = send('engine', 'analyze', { url, context: ctx || {} })
      .then((res) => {
        const v = res.ok ? res.data : { verdict: 'CLEAR', reasons: [],
                                        score: 0, meta: { error: res.error } };
        verdictPending.delete(key);
        verdictCache.set(key, v);
        if (verdictCache.size > VERDICT_CACHE_MAX) {
          verdictCache.delete(verdictCache.keys().next().value);
        }
        return v;
      });
    verdictPending.set(key, p);
    return p;
  }

  /* ---- status lines shown in the popup ---------------------------- */
  function status(moduleId, line) {
    // fire-and-forget; SW writes session storage keyed by tab id
    send(moduleId, 'status', { line: String(line).slice(0, 80) });
  }

  /* ---- lifecycle ---------------------------------------------------- */
  async function enabledFor(moduleId) {
    const s = await root.Settings.get();
    return root.Settings.isModuleOn(s, moduleId, pageHost);
  }

  async function configFor(moduleId) {
    const s = await root.Settings.effective(pageHost);
    return s.modules[moduleId] || { enabled: false };
  }

  function registerCleanup(moduleId, fn) {
    if (typeof fn !== 'function') return;
    (cleanups[moduleId] = cleanups[moduleId] || []).push(fn);
  }

  function teardown(moduleId) {
    const fns = cleanups[moduleId] || [];
    cleanups[moduleId] = [];
    for (const fn of fns) {
      try { fn(); } catch (e) { /* undo must never throw the bridge */ }
    }
    alive[moduleId] = false;
    const impl = impls[moduleId];
    if (impl && typeof impl.onDisabled === 'function') {



SecondLook - Build Guide                                                          17
          try { impl.onDisabled(); } catch (e) { /* ignore */ }
      }
  }

  async function boot(moduleId, impl) {
    impls[moduleId] = impl || {};
    const on = await enabledFor(moduleId);
    if (on) {
      alive[moduleId] = true;
      const cfg = await configFor(moduleId);
      if (typeof impl.onEnabled === 'function') {
        impl.onEnabled(cfg);   // module starts observers here
      }
    }
  }

  /* ---- broadcast + settings listeners (installed once) ------------- */
  chrome.runtime.onMessage.addListener((msg) => {
    if (!msg || typeof msg !== 'object') return false;
    if (msg.module !== '*') return false;
    const p = msg.payload || {};
    if (msg.type === 'moduleToggled' && impls[p.moduleId]) {
      if (p.enabled === true) {
        (async () => {
          alive[p.moduleId] = true;
          const cfg = await configFor(p.moduleId);
          const impl = impls[p.moduleId];
          if (typeof impl.onEnabled === 'function') impl.onEnabled(cfg);
        })();
      } else {
        teardown(p.moduleId);    // run undo fns, then onDisabled()
      }
    } else if (msg.type === 'globalToggled') {
      if (p.enabled === false) {
        for (const id of Object.keys(impls)) teardown(id);
      }
    } else if (msg.type === 'moduleConfig' && impls[p.moduleId]) {
      const impl = impls[p.moduleId];
      if (typeof impl.onConfig === 'function') impl.onConfig(p.config || {});
    }
    return false;   // broadcasts never expect a response
  });

  /* Local settings edits (options page) reach content scripts through
   * storage.onChanged; re-check enablement and config for our modules. */
  chrome.storage.onChanged.addListener(async (changes, area) => {
    if (area !== 'local' || !changes['sl.settings']) return;
    for (const moduleId of Object.keys(impls)) {
      const on = await enabledFor(moduleId);
      if (on && !alive[moduleId]) {
        alive[moduleId] = true;
        const cfg = await configFor(moduleId);
        const impl = impls[moduleId];
        if (typeof impl.onEnabled === 'function') impl.onEnabled(cfg);
      } else if (!on && alive[moduleId]) {
        teardown(moduleId);
      } else if (on && alive[moduleId]) {
        const cfg = await configFor(moduleId);
        const impl = impls[moduleId];
        if (typeof impl.onConfig === 'function') impl.onConfig(cfg);
      }
    }
  });

  root.__bridge = true;
  root.host = host;
  root.send = send;
  root.verdict = verdict;
  root.status = status;
  root.boot = boot;
  root.registerCleanup = registerCleanup;
  root.teardown = teardown;
})(globalThis);




SecondLook - Build Guide                                                        18
0.8 The engine
Five files form the verdict engine, and they are the only code in the product allowed to produce risk numbers.
url-analyzer.js extracts weighted signals; known-brands.js and domain-intel.js feed it; redirect-resolver.js
(service-worker-only) follows redirect chains; verdict-engine.js combines everything into the three-word verdict. All
engine exports carry JSDoc, because every module in Parts 1-10 consumes this API and the contract must be unambiguous.


engine/known-brands.js
The brand list is the reference data behind typo-squatting detection. Each entry maps a brand token to its official registrable
domains. Detection runs in two modes: exact (the site's registrable domain is a listed official domain of a brand it claims to
be, or the registrable label equals the brand token) and fuzzy (the registrable label is within Damerau-Levenshtein distance 2
of a brand token - paypa1, micros0ft, arnazon). The list is deliberately conservative: brands people actually type and trust,
not a directory of the internet.

  engine/known-brands.js
/* engine/known-brands.js - brand tokens + official domains.

  const DOMAIN_SET = new Set();
  const TOKEN_MAP = new Map();
  for (const entry of KNOWN_BRANDS) {
    TOKEN_MAP.set(entry.brand, entry);
    for (const d of entry.domains) DOMAIN_SET.add(d);
  }
  /** True when host's registrable domain is an official brand domain. */



SecondLook - Build Guide                                                    20
  function isOfficialDomain(registrable) {
    return DOMAIN_SET.has(registrable);
  }

  /** Exact brand whose token equals the registrable label, if any. */
  function brandForToken(label) {
    return TOKEN_MAP.get(label) || null;
  }

  /** Damerau-Levenshtein distance, capped for speed. */
  function damerau(a, bToken, cap) {
    const la = a.length, lb = bToken.length;
    if (Math.abs(la - lb) > cap) return cap + 1;
    const d = Array.from({ length: la + 1 }, () => new Array(lb + 1).fill(0));
    for (let i = 0; i <= la; i++) d[i][0] = i;
    for (let j = 0; j <= lb; j++) d[0][j] = j;
    for (let i = 1; i <= la; i++) {
      for (let j = 1; j <= lb; j++) {
        const cost = a[i - 1] === bToken[j - 1] ? 0 : 1;
        d[i][j] = Math.min(d[i - 1][j] + 1, d[i][j - 1] + 1,
                           d[i - 1][j - 1] + cost);
        if (i > 1 && j > 1 && a[i - 1] === bToken[j - 2] &&
            a[i - 2] === bToken[j - 1]) {
          d[i][j] = Math.min(d[i][j], d[i - 2][j - 2] + 1); // transposition
        }
      }
    }
    return d[la][lb];
  }

  /** Brand whose token is within edit distance 2 of label (typo-squat). */
  function typoBrandFor(label) {
    if (!label || label.length < 4) return null;
    const cap = label.length <= 4 ? 1 : 2;
    for (const entry of KNOWN_BRANDS) {
      if (Math.abs(entry.brand.length - label.length) > cap) continue;
      if (damerau(label, entry.brand, cap) <= cap) return entry;
    }
    return null;
  }

  root.Engine.Brands = {
     KNOWN_BRANDS, isOfficialDomain, brandForToken, typoBrandFor, damerau
  };
})(globalThis);



engine/domain-intel.js
Domain intelligence starts with registrable-domain extraction, which needs Public Suffix List awareness. Bundling the full
14,000-entry PSL would be correct but heavy for a guide; instead we ship a curated subset of roughly eighty multi-label
suffixes that covers effectively all traffic a real user hits (co.uk, com.au, co.jp, com.br, ...), plus a documented upgrade
path. The remaining helpers classify IP literals, count subdomain depth, detect hyphen stuffing, and recognize
machine-generated-looking labels - each a named function so the analyzer stays readable.

  engine/domain-intel.js
/* engine/domain-intel.js - registrable domains + host shape helpers. */
(function (global) {
  'use strict';
  const root = global.SL = global.SL || {};
  root.Engine = root.Engine || {};
  if (root.Engine.Domain) return;

  /* Curated multi-label public suffixes (subset of the PSL).
   * Single-label TLDs (com, net, org, io, ...) need no entry here. */
  const MULTI_SUFFIXES = new Set([
    'co.uk', 'org.uk', 'ac.uk', 'gov.uk', 'me.uk', 'net.uk', 'ltd.uk',
    'com.au', 'net.au', 'org.au', 'gov.au', 'edu.au', 'id.au',
    'co.nz', 'net.nz', 'org.nz', 'govt.nz', 'ac.nz',
    'co.jp', 'or.jp', 'ne.jp', 'ac.jp', 'go.jp',
    'co.za', 'org.za', 'net.za', 'web.za', 'gov.za',



SecondLook - Build Guide                                                                                                 21
    'com.br', 'net.br', 'org.br', 'gov.br', 'edu.br',
    'com.mx', 'org.mx', 'net.mx', 'gob.mx', 'edu.mx',
    'com.ar', 'net.ar', 'org.ar', 'gob.ar', 'edu.ar',
    'com.cn', 'net.cn', 'org.cn', 'gov.cn', 'edu.cn',
    'com.hk', 'net.hk', 'org.hk', 'gov.hk', 'edu.hk',
    'com.tw', 'net.tw', 'org.tw', 'gov.tw', 'edu.tw',
    'com.sg', 'net.sg', 'org.sg', 'gov.sg', 'edu.sg',
    'com.my', 'net.my', 'org.my', 'gov.my', 'edu.my',
    'co.in', 'net.in', 'org.in', 'gov.in', 'ac.in', 'edu.in',
    'com.tr', 'net.tr', 'org.tr', 'gov.tr', 'edu.tr',
    'com.ua', 'net.ua', 'org.ua', 'gov.ua', 'edu.ua',
    'co.il', 'org.il', 'net.il', 'gov.il', 'ac.il',
    'com.ru', 'net.ru', 'org.ru',
    'co.kr', 'or.kr', 'ne.kr', 'go.kr',
    'com.vn', 'net.vn', 'org.vn', 'gov.vn',
    'com.ph', 'net.ph', 'org.ph',
    'com.co', 'net.co', 'org.co', 'gov.co',
    'com.pe', 'org.pe', 'net.pe', 'gob.pe',
    'com.ve', 'net.ve', 'org.ve', 'gob.ve',
    'com.eg', 'net.eg', 'org.eg', 'gov.eg',
    'com.sa', 'net.sa', 'org.sa', 'gov.sa',
    'com.ae', 'net.ae', 'org.ae', 'gov.ae',
    'com.qa', 'net.qa', 'org.qa', 'gov.qa',
    'com.kw', 'net.kw', 'org.kw',
    'com.pk', 'net.pk', 'org.pk', 'gov.pk',
    'com.bd', 'net.bd', 'org.bd',
    'com.ng', 'net.ng', 'org.ng', 'gov.ng',
    'co.ke', 'or.ke', 'go.ke', 'ac.ke',
    'com.gh', 'org.gh', 'gov.gh',
    'com.th', 'net.th', 'org.th', 'go.th',
    'co.th', 'in.th', 'or.th', 'go.th'
  ]);

  function splitHost(hostname) {
    return String(hostname || '').toLowerCase().replace(/\.$/, '').split('.');
  }

  /** eTLD+1: 'login.paypal.co.uk' -> 'paypal.co.uk'. Falls back to the
    * full host when nothing matches (single-label or odd TLD). */
  function eTLDPlus1(hostname) {
     const labels = splitHost(hostname);
     if (labels.length <= 2) return labels.join('.');
     // longest matching multi-label suffix wins
     for (let take = 3; take <= Math.min(labels.length, 5); take++) {
       const suffix = labels.slice(-take).join('.');
       if (MULTI_SUFFIXES.has(suffix)) {
         return labels.slice(-(take + 1)).join('.');
       }
     }
     return labels.slice(-2).join('.');
  }

  /** The brand-carrying label of the registrable domain. */
  function registrableLabel(hostname) {
    const reg = eTLDPlus1(hostname);
    return reg.split('.')[0] || reg;
  }

  function isIpLiteral(host) {
    if (!host) return false;
    if (/^\d{1,3}(\.\d{1,3}){3}$/.test(host)) return true;       // IPv4
    if (host.startsWith('[') && host.endsWith(']')) return true; // IPv6
    if (host.includes(':') && host.includes('::')) return true; // bare IPv6
    return false;
  }

  /** Labels to the left of the registrable domain. */
  function subdomainDepth(hostname) {
    const labels = splitHost(hostname);
    const regLabels = eTLDPlus1(hostname).split('.').length;
    return Math.max(0, labels.length - regLabels);
  }

  function hyphenCount(str) {



SecondLook - Build Guide                                                         22
      return (String(str).match(/-/g) || []).length;
  }

  /** Heuristic for DGA-style labels: long + almost vowel-free. */
  function looksMachineGenerated(label) {
    if (!label || label.length < 13) return false;
    const vowels = (label.match(/[aeiou]/g) || []).length;
    return vowels / label.length < 0.30;
  }

  root.Engine.Domain = {
     MULTI_SUFFIXES, splitHost, eTLDPlus1, registrableLabel,
     isIpLiteral, subdomainDepth, hyphenCount, looksMachineGenerated
  };
})(globalThis);



engine/url-analyzer.js
The analyzer turns one URL (plus optional context) into a list of weighted signals. Every weight is a named constant; every
signal carries a plainText sentence written for a non-technical reader, because the UI renders exactly that sentence and
nothing else. Additive scoring with a cap happens in the verdict engine; this file only reports what it sees.

  engine/url-analyzer.js
/* engine/url-analyzer.js - weighted signal extraction for one URL. */
(function (global) {
  'use strict';
  const root = global.SL = global.SL || {};
  root.Engine = root.Engine || {};
  if (root.Engine.Url) return;

  /* All weights are named constants - the tuning knobs of the engine.
   * Any single URL-only combination must stay below the engine's
   * URL_CAP (39) so pure URL shape can never trigger INTERCEPTED. */
  const SIGNAL_WEIGHTS = {
     PUNYCODE_IDN: 45,
     BRAND_TYPO: 40,
     BRAND_IN_SUBDOMAIN: 30,
     INVISIBLE_CHARS: 50,
     IP_LITERAL: 30,
     USERINFO_URL: 25,
     NONSTD_PORT: 15,
     SUSPICIOUS_KEYWORD: 15,
     SUSPICIOUS_TLD: 12,
     DEEP_SUBDOMAINS: 12,
     HYPHEN_STUFFED: 10,
     LONG_RANDOM_LABEL: 10,
     KNOWN_SHORTENER: 10,
     PLAIN_HTTP: 8
  };

  const SUSPICIOUS_TLDS = [
     'zip', 'mov', 'top', 'gq', 'tk', 'cf', 'ml', 'ga', 'work', 'click',
     'link', 'country', 'stream', 'download', 'loan', 'review', 'icu',
     'cam', 'rest', 'bs'
  ];

  const SHORTENERS = [
     'bit.ly', 't.co', 'tinyurl.com', 'is.gd', 'cutt.ly', 'shorturl.at',
     'rebrand.ly', 'buff.ly', 'ow.ly', 's.id', 'v.gd', 'rb.gy', 'tiny.cc',
     'shrtco.de', 't.ly', 'soo.gd', 'clck.ru', 'shorte.st', 'gg.gg',
     'short.gy', 'budurl.com', 'qr.ae'
  ];

  const HARVEST_KEYWORDS = [
     'login', 'signin', 'verify', 'verification', 'secure', 'account',
     'update', 'confirm', 'recovery', 'wallet', 'billing', 'invoice',
     'password', 'support'
  ];

  /* Zero-width + bidi control characters that can disguise link text
   * or hostnames when rendered. Checked in both display text and URL. */



SecondLook - Build Guide                                                                                                 23
  const INVISIBLE_RE = /[\u200b-\u200f\u202a-\u202e\u2060-\u2064\u2066-\u2069\ufeff]/;

  function analyzeUrl(url, context) {
    const ctx = context || {};
    const signals = [];
    const add = (signal, plainText) => signals.push({
      signal, weight: SIGNAL_WEIGHTS[signal] || 0, plainText
    });

     let u = null;
     try { u = new URL(url); } catch (e) {
       add('PUNYCODE_IDN', 'This is not a well-formed address.');
       return { signals, host: '', registrable: '', meta: { parsed: false } };
     }

     const host = (u.hostname || '').toLowerCase();
     const D = root.Engine.Domain, B = root.Engine.Brands;
     const registrable = D.eTLDPlus1(host);
     const regLabel = D.registrableLabel(host);
     const labels = D.splitHost(host);

     // 1. punycode / non-ASCII host
     if (host.startsWith('xn--') || labels.some((l) => l.startsWith('xn--')) ||
         /[^\x00-\x7f]/.test(host)) {
       add('PUNYCODE_IDN',
           'The address contains encoded characters that can be used ' +
           'to imitate real sites.');
     }

     // 2. typo-squat of a known brand
     if (!D.isIpLiteral(host) && !B.isOfficialDomain(registrable)) {
       const typo = B.typoBrandFor(regLabel);
       if (typo) {
         add('BRAND_TYPO', 'Looks similar to ' + typo.brand +
             '.com - the official domain is ' + typo.domains[0] + '.');
       }
     }

     // 3. brand name parked in a subdomain of someone else
     if (!B.isOfficialDomain(registrable)) {
       for (const label of labels.slice(0, -2)) {
         const exact = B.brandForToken(label);
         if (exact) {
           add('BRAND_IN_SUBDOMAIN', 'Uses \'' + exact.brand +
               '\' in the subdomain, but the site is really ' +
               registrable + '.');
           break;
         }
       }
     }

     // 4. invisible characters in display text (spec: text vs href)
     if (ctx.displayText && INVISIBLE_RE.test(ctx.displayText)) {
       add('INVISIBLE_CHARS',
           'The visible link text contains invisible characters that ' +
           'can disguise where it leads.');
     }

     // 5. raw IP host
     if (D.isIpLiteral(host)) {
       add('IP_LITERAL',
           'Points to a raw numeric address instead of a named website.');
     }

     // 6. userinfo trick - http://paypal.com@evil.example/
     if (u.username || u.password) {
       add('USERINFO_URL',
           'Everything before the \'@\' in this address is decoration; ' +
           'the real destination is after it.');
     }

     // 7. unusual port
     const port = u.port ? Number(u.port) : null;
     if (port && port !== 80 && port !== 443 && port !== 8080) {



SecondLook - Build Guide                                                                 24
          add('NONSTD_PORT', 'Uses an unusual port number (' + port + ').');
      }

      // 8. keyword-stuffed registrable label
      if (!D.isIpLiteral(host)) {
        for (const kw of HARVEST_KEYWORDS) {
          if (regLabel.includes(kw) && regLabel !== kw) {
            add('SUSPICIOUS_KEYWORD', 'The domain name contains \'' + kw +
                '\', typical of credential-harvesting sites.');
            break;
          }
        }
      }

      // 9. TLD reputation
      const tld = labels[labels.length - 1];
      if (SUSPICIOUS_TLDS.includes(tld)) {
        add('SUSPICIOUS_TLD',
            'Uses the .' + tld + ' ending, which is common in scam sites.');
      }

      // 10. subdomain depth
      if (D.subdomainDepth(host) >= 4) {
        add('DEEP_SUBDOMAINS',
            'Unusually deep subdomains can imitate official site names.');
      }

      // 11. hyphen stuffing
      if (D.hyphenCount(regLabel) >= 3) {
        add('HYPHEN_STUFFED',
            'The domain name packs together many dashes, a disguise pattern.');
      }

      // 12. machine-generated label
      if (D.looksMachineGenerated(regLabel)) {
        add('LONG_RANDOM_LABEL',
            'The domain name looks machine-generated rather than human-named.');
      }

      // 13. shortener
      if (SHORTENERS.includes(registrable)) {
        add('KNOWN_SHORTENER',
            'Shortened link - its destination is not shown.');
      }

      // 14. plain http
      if (u.protocol === 'http:') {
        add('PLAIN_HTTP', 'The connection is not encrypted (http).');
      }

      return {
         signals,
         host, registrable,
         meta: {
           parsed: true, protocol: u.protocol.replace(':', ''),
           isShortener: SHORTENERS.includes(registrable),
           port, labels
         }
      };
  }

  root.Engine.Url = { SIGNAL_WEIGHTS, analyzeUrl, SHORTENERS };
})(globalThis);



engine/verdict-engine.js
The verdict engine folds one or two URL analyses (the original, and optionally a resolved final URL for shortlinks) into a
single verdict object. The safety cap is the heart of the design: the summed URL-only score is clamped at 39 while
interception requires 40, so no pile-up of heuristics - however paranoid - can stop a user who has not been presented with hard
module-level evidence. Only a module passing escalate: true (Form Guardian with credentials in flight, Download Guard
with byte-level mismatch) can legally cross that line.


SecondLook - Build Guide                                                                                                     25
  engine/verdict-engine.js
/* engine/verdict-engine.js - the ONLY place a verdict is produced. */
(function (global) {
  'use strict';
  const root = global.SL = global.SL || {};
  root.Engine = root.Engine || {};
  if (root.Engine.analyze) return;

  const THRESHOLDS = {
     SECOND_LOOK: 20,  // score at or above this earns an amber pill
     INTERCEPT: 40,    // never reachable from URL signals alone
     URL_CAP: 39       // hard clamp for URL-only analysis
  };

  const VERDICT = {
     CLEAR: 'CLEAR',
     SECOND_LOOK: 'SECOND_LOOK',
     INTERCEPTED: 'INTERCEPTED'
  };

  /**
   * Analyze a URL and produce a SecondLook verdict.
   * @param {string} url - the URL to judge.
   * @param {object} [context] - extra evidence from the calling module:
   *    displayText visible text of the link/anchor, if any
   *    credentials true when credentials are about to be sent (P4)
   *    escalate     true when the module has hard evidence and is
   *                 allowed to request INTERCEPTED
   *    resolvedUrl final URL after redirect resolution (P1/P2)
   * @returns {Promise<{verdict:string, reasons:Array, score:number,
   *                     meta:object}>} reasons are plain-language,
   *                     strongest first, already capped to three.
   */
  function analyze(url, context) {
     const ctx = context || {};
     const Url = root.Engine.Url;

     const first = Url.analyzeUrl(url, ctx);
     const signals = first.signals.slice();
     let meta = Object.assign({}, first.meta, { host: first.host });

     // Shortlinks: judge the resolved destination too, if provided.
     if (ctx.resolvedUrl && ctx.resolvedUrl !== url) {
       const second = Url.analyzeUrl(ctx.resolvedUrl, ctx);
       for (const sig of second.signals) {
         if (sig.signal === 'KNOWN_SHORTENER') continue;
         const dupe = signals.find((s) => s.signal === sig.signal);
         if (!dupe) signals.push(sig);
       }
       meta.resolvedHost = second.host;
     }

     // Score: additive, then clamped for URL-only analysis.
     let score = 0;
     for (const sig of signals) score += sig.weight;
     if (!ctx.escalate) score = Math.min(score, THRESHOLDS.URL_CAP);
     else score = Math.min(score, 100);

     let verdict;
     if (ctx.escalate && ctx.credentials && score >= THRESHOLDS.INTERCEPT) {
       verdict = VERDICT.INTERCEPTED;
     } else if (score >= THRESHOLDS.SECOND_LOOK) {
       verdict = VERDICT.SECOND_LOOK;
     } else {
       verdict = VERDICT.CLEAR;
     }

     // One visible warning per event: top reasons only, plain text.
     const reasons = signals
       .filter((s) => s.weight >= 10)
       .sort((a, b) => b.weight - a.weight)
       .slice(0, 3)
       .map((s) => s.plainText);



SecondLook - Build Guide                                                       26
      return Promise.resolve({ verdict, reasons, score, meta });
  }

  root.Engine.analyze = analyze;
  root.Engine.VERDICT = VERDICT;
  root.Engine.THRESHOLDS = THRESHOLDS;
})(globalThis);



engine/redirect-resolver.js
Redirect resolution is where MV3 bites everyone, so this file exists to bite back. The naive approach - fetch(url,
{redirect: 'manual'}) and reading Location - does not work: Chromium returns an *opaque filtered response* whose
headers are unreadable, by design. The working approach is the opposite shape: issue a normal *following* fetch from the
service     worker         (which   host     permissions   allow   cross-origin),   and   observe   the   hop   chain   through
webRequest.onBeforeRedirect events keyed by requestId. The final URL comes from response.url as ground truth.
Resolutions are serialized through a promise queue so concurrent callers can never mix up request ids.

  engine/redirect-resolver.js
/* engine/redirect-resolver.js - SERVICE WORKER ONLY (needs webRequest).
 *
 * The trap this file exists to avoid:
 *    fetch(url, {redirect:'manual'}) -> OPAQUE response, headers
 *    unreadable. Useless for chain inspection.
 * The working shape: follow redirects with fetch(), capture each hop
 * via webRequest.onBeforeRedirect keyed by requestId. */
(function (global) {
   'use strict';
   const root = global.SL = global.SL || {};
   root.Engine = root.Engine || {};
   if (root.Engine.Redirect) return;

  const MAX_HOPS = 8;                      // tuning: stop following after this many hops
  const TIMEOUT_MS = 6000;                 // tuning: per-resolution budget

  let queue = Promise.resolve();                // serialize: one resolution in flight
  let collector = null;                         // requestId -> [{fromUrl,toUrl,status}]
  let listening = false;

  function ensureListener() {
    if (listening) return;
    listening = true;
    // OBSERVER ONLY: no blocking, no header modification.
    chrome.webRequest.onBeforeRedirect.addListener((details) => {
      if (!collector) return;
      const list = collector.get(details.requestId) || [];
      list.push({
        fromUrl: details.url,
        toUrl: details.redirectUrl,
        status: details.statusCode
      });
      collector.set(details.requestId, list);
    }, { urls: ['<all_urls>'] });
  }

  function buildChain(startUrl, events, finalUrl) {
    const chain = [startUrl];
    let cursor = startUrl;
    for (const ev of events) {
      if (ev.fromUrl === cursor) { chain.push(ev.toUrl); cursor = ev.toUrl; }
    }
    if (finalUrl && chain[chain.length - 1] !== finalUrl) {
      chain.push(finalUrl);
    }
    return chain;
  }

  async function resolveOnce(url) {
    collector = new Map();
    ensureListener();
    const ctrl = new AbortController();


SecondLook - Build Guide                                                                                                     27
      const timer = setTimeout(() => ctrl.abort(), TIMEOUT_MS);
      let finalUrl = url;
      let error = null;
      try {
         const res = await fetch(url, {
           redirect: 'follow',
           credentials: 'omit',   // never send the user's cookies upstream
           cache: 'no-store',
           signal: ctrl.signal
         });
         finalUrl = res.url || url;
      } catch (err) {
         error = String((err && err.message) || err);
      } finally {
         clearTimeout(timer);
      }
      // Pick the event list that starts at our URL; prefer one that also
      // ends at our finalUrl (guards against unrelated parallel requests).
      let events = null;
      for (const list of collector.values()) {
         if (!list.length || list[0].fromUrl !== url) continue;
         if (!events) events = list;
         if (finalUrl && list[list.length - 1].toUrl === finalUrl) { events = list; break; }
      }
      collector = null;
      const chain = buildChain(url, events || [], finalUrl)
         .slice(0, MAX_HOPS + 1);
      return {
         chain,
         finalUrl,
         hops: Math.max(0, chain.length - 1),
         error
      };
  }

  /**
    * Resolve a URL's redirect chain.
    * @param {string} url
    * @returns {Promise<{chain:string[], finalUrl:string, hops:number,
    *                     error:(string|null)}>}
    */
  function resolve(url) {
      const run = queue.then(() => resolveOnce(url));
      queue = run.then(() => undefined, () => undefined); // keep queue alive
      return run;
  }

  root.Engine.Redirect = { resolve, MAX_HOPS, TIMEOUT_MS };
})(globalThis);


   DEVIATION NOTE - The domain-intel suffix list is a curated subset of the Public Suffix List, not the full PSL. Rare multi-label
   suffixes (e.g. some ccTLD second-level zones) may mis-split. Upgrading later is a data swap: replace MULTI_SUFFIXES with a
   generated Set from the full public_suffix_list.dat and keep the same function signatures.


   TIP - Keep an eye on the engine's unit behaviors with the DevTools console of the service worker (chrome://extensions ->
   SecondLook -> "service worker"). After Part 0 loads you can type
   SL.Engine.analyze('http://paypa1-login.com/verify') in that console and watch the verdict object come back -
   the fastest sanity check in the whole project.




0.9 The design system
The UI kit is two stylesheets and two scripts, and it is the only visual vocabulary every module is allowed to speak.
tokens.css defines every color, spacing, radius, font size and z-index as CSS custom properties, in both light and dark
mode, driven by prefers-color-scheme. components.css implements the shared component classes: verdict pill, toggle
switch, module row, card, toast, banner, intercept layer, indicator chip, stat tile and empty state. toast.js and


SecondLook - Build Guide                                                                                                             28
verdict-card.js are their behavioral halves, written as classic scripts so the identical files run in content worlds and
extension pages.

Three rules from the product spec are enforced here rather than hoped for later. No gradients anywhere - flat surfaces plus a
1px border plus a soft shadow. No emoji ever - the only icons are inline SVG strokes. And every interactive element is a real
button or a with a visible :focus-visible ring, keyboard dismissal on Escape, and animations that switch off entirely
under prefers-reduced-motion. Contrast targets are WCAG AA: verdict text colors are chosen at 4.5:1 or better on their
surfaces in both modes.


ui/tokens.css
